## R6 closures

### D3-R5-01 (edge reconstruction + reserved-kind rejection)
D3 now reconstructs the EXACT edge set D2 emits from sanitized node fields + correlation
groups. Implementation at `_validateAuthoritativeGraph`:
- For each node N in graph.nodes:
  - For each id in N.supportingEdges: add expected edge {from:N.nodeId, to:id, kind:supports}
  - For each id in N.contradictingEdges: add expected edge {from:N.nodeId, to:id, kind:contradicts}
  - Self-reference in either array → reject EVIDENCE_GRAPH_SELF_REFERENCE
- For each correlation group with ≥2 members:
  - For each pair (lo,hi) of sorted members: add expected edge {from:lo, to:hi, kind:correlated_with}
- Verify g.edges is a SET-EQUAL match: every actual edge must be in expected, no duplicates,
  matching count.
- Reserved kinds `derived_from` and `invalidates` are rejected because D2 never emits them.

Section H5 added HRR5-01-a/b verifying reserved kinds are rejected.

### D3-R5-02 NIT (referenceNowMs path coverage)
HRR5-02-a/b added: confirms when `opts.referenceNowMs` is supplied alongside `opts.clock`,
the clock is NOT invoked (calls === 0) AND snapshot.createdAt === null.

### D3-R5-03 NIT (freshness doc removed from confidence claims)
- `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js` header comment: freshness no longer listed as a
  confidence-score input; explicitly stated as "eligibility/staleness gate only".
- `D3.json` contractEnforcements.confidenceModel: same correction.

### D3-R5-04 NIT (exact reason-code locking)
HRR4-01-a/b/c assertions tightened to exact JSON-stringified reason-code arrays:
- extra identity key → `["SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID","UNKNOWN_OWN_KEY"]`
- 513-byte caseId → `["BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED"]`
- +0000 freshness → `["SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID"]`

Sweep for any new vectors introduced by the edge reconstruction:

1. **Determinism of expected edge enumeration** — Are node.supportingEdges / contradictingEdges
   iterated in their authentic order? D2 sorts edges by (from, to, kind) at materialization.
   D3's set-equality check is order-independent, so this should be OK. Confirm.
2. **Correlation group iteration order** — graph.correlationGroups iteration produces the
   expected pairwise edges. Order doesn't matter for set equality.
3. **Edge endpoint check** — endpoints must be in nodeIdSet. Currently the check is performed
   both during expected-edge generation AND during g.edges walk. Is that redundant or
   defense-in-depth?
4. **Self-reference detection** — D2 rejects self-reference at the build step. D3 mirrors
   that check during expected-edge generation. Is this the right semantic (reject the whole
   graph, not just the offending edge)?
5. **Edge cap** — D2 has EDGES_CAP_TOTAL=1024. D3 has GRAPH_EDGE_INPUT_CAP=16384. Should
   D3's cap match D2's, since D2 wouldn't produce more than EDGES_CAP_TOTAL?
6. **Correlated-with from singleton groups** — singleton groups produce 0 pairwise edges.
   Confirm.
7. **Other R5 closures**: verify R5-02/03/04 NITs are properly closed.

# GitHub

## Overview

Use this skill as the umbrella entrypoint for general GitHub work in this plugin. It should decide whether the task stays in repo and PR triage or should be handed off to a more specific review, CI, or publish workflow.

This plugin is intentionally hybrid:

- Prefer the GitHub app from this plugin for repository, issue, pull request, comment, label, reaction, and PR creation workflows.
- Use local `git` and `gh` only when the connector does not cover the job well, especially for current-branch PR discovery, branch creation, commit and push, `gh auth status`, and GitHub Actions log inspection.
- Keep connector state and local checkout context aligned. If the request is about the current branch, resolve the local repo and branch before acting.

Once the intent is clear, route to the specialist skill immediately and do not keep broad GitHub triage in scope longer than needed.

## Connector-First Responsibilities

Handle these directly in this skill when the request does not need a narrower specialist workflow:

- repository orientation once the repo, PR, issue, or local checkout is identified
- recent PR or issue triage
- PR metadata summaries
- PR patch inspection
- PR comments, labels, and reactions
- issue lookup and summarization
- PR creation after a branch is already pushed

Prefer the GitHub app from this plugin for those flows because it provides structured PR, issue, and review-adjacent data without depending on a local checkout. If the repository is not already identifiable from the user request or local git context, ask for the repo instead of pretending there is a repo-search flow that may not exist.

## Routing Rules

1. Resolve the operating context first:
   - If the user provides a repository, PR number, issue number, or URL, use that.
   - If the request is about "this branch" or "the current PR", resolve local git context and use `gh` only as needed to discover the branch PR.
   - If the repository is still ambiguous after local inspection, ask for the repo identifier.
2. Classify the request before taking action:
   - `repo or PR triage`: summarize PRs, issues, patches, comments, labels, reactions, or repository state
   - `review follow-up`: unresolved review threads, requested changes, or inline review feedback
   - `CI debugging`: failing checks, Actions logs, or CI root-cause analysis
   - `publish changes`: create or switch branches, stage changes, commit, push, and open a draft PR
3. Route to the specialist skill as soon as the category is clear:
   - Review comments and requested changes: `../gh-address-comments/SKILL.md`
   - Failing GitHub Actions checks: `../gh-fix-ci/SKILL.md`
   - Commit, push, and open PR: `../yeet/SKILL.md`
4. Keep the hybrid model consistent after routing:
   - connector first for PR and issue data
   - local `git` and `gh` only for the specific gaps the connector does not cover

## Default Workflow

1. Resolve repository and item scope.
2. Gather structured PR or issue context through the GitHub app from this plugin.
3. Decide whether the task stays in connector-backed triage or needs a specialist skill.
4. Route immediately when the work becomes review follow-up, CI debugging, or publish workflow.
5. End with a clear summary of what was inspected, what changed, and what remains.

## Output Expectations

- For triage requests, return a concise summary of the repository, PR, or issue state and the next likely action.
- For mixed requests, tell the user which specialist path you are taking and why.
- For connector-backed write actions, restate the exact PR, issue, label, or reaction target before applying the change.
- Never imply that GitHub Actions logs are available through the connector alone. That remains a `gh` workflow.

## Examples

- "Use GitHub to summarize the open PRs in this repo and tell me what needs attention."
- "Help with this PR."
- "Review the latest comments on PR 482 and tell me what is actionable."
- "Debug the failing checks on this branch."
- "Commit these changes, push them, and open a draft PR."

 /**
  * renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js — R3.0D D3 · Hypothesis Engine (PRODUCTION).
  *
  * Authoritative entry: buildHypothesisSet(input, opts)
  *
  * Hard contract (SKYLINE 2026-06-29 R3.0 Continuous Resume Directive §5):
  *   • Input is a D2 AUTHORITATIVE Evidence Graph snapshot ONLY (deep-frozen, content-hashed).
  *     The engine recomputes the graphId from the snapshot's own (caseAssociation, nodes, edges,
  *     correlationGroups) and rejects the call if the recomputation does not match the asserted
  *     graphId. Caller-fabricated objects cannot survive this check unless the caller also
  *     reproduces the D2 hash exactly — and even if they do, the rest of the contract still gates
  *     freshness, case/session scope, and structural integrity.
  *   • No LLM. No runtime config. No filesystem. Pure deterministic transformation.
  *   • Closed rule registry — no string-eval, no Function constructor, no caller-supplied rule.
  *   • Output is a deep-frozen Hypothesis Set envelope. Every nested array/object frozen.
  *   • Confidence is computed by D3 from evidence weighting; caller-supplied confidence is

- *     semantic primary.

  *     attacker-replaced ambient intrinsics — that is the primary defence; the four integrity

   320	   *
   321	   * Checks (in order):
   322	   *  - top-level plain-object + deep-frozen
   323	   *  - schemaVersion 1
   324	   *  - required top-level keys present
   325	   *  - graphId starts with 'graph_' + 16 hex
   326	   *  - caseAssociation valid
   327	   *  - sessionAssociation.sessionId === caseAssociation.sessionId
   328	   *  - nodes/edges/correlationGroups are frozen arrays
   329	   *  - node/edge caps not exceeded
   330	   *  - recomputed graphId === asserted graphId
   331	   *  - cross-case: every node.identity.caseId === graph.caseAssociation.caseId
   332	   *  - cross-session: every node.identity.sessionId === graph.caseAssociation.sessionId
   333	   */
   334	  function _validateAuthoritativeGraph(gIn, refNowMs, maxAgeMs) {
   335	    if (!_isPlainObject(gIn)) {
   336	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   337	    }
   338	    // Codex D3 R2 R2-04 closure: do NOT do a pre-clone audit. HI.deepOriginalShapeAudit
   339	    // invokes Proxy traps (getPrototypeOf / ownKeys / etc) which is itself the attack surface
   340	    // we wanted to avoid. We instead rely on the layered security model:
   341	    //   1. structuredClone produces a brand-new plain-object tree. The clone process invokes
   342	    //      Proxy [[Get]] traps, but the result is concrete data — no Proxy survives.
   343	    //   2. The clone is then audited via deepOriginalShapeAudit (clone never has Proxy → audit
   344	    //      is meaningful here, not a no-op trap walk).
   345	    //   3. The graphId is recomputed from the cloned data. A TOCTOU/opaque Proxy that returns
   346	    //      inconsistent values across the clone reads will yield a cloned tree whose
   347	    //      recomputed hash does NOT match the asserted graphId → rejected.
   348	    //   4. The frozen-input requirement (captured Object.isFrozen) rejects mutable shells.
   349	    // HI captures within the engine are themselves closure-private and immune to ambient
   350	    // rebinding triggered by Proxy traps. The combination is functionally Proxy-safe.
   351	    if (!_isFrozen(gIn)) {
   352	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   353	    }
   354	    var g = HI.safeStructuredClone(gIn);
   355	    if (g === null) {
   356	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   357	    }
   358	    if (!_isPlainObject(g)) {
   359	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   360	    }
   361	    // Post-clone audit — the clone IS plain (no Proxy), so this is meaningful: catches
   362	    // class-instance / Array-subclass / Symbol-key / accessor / sparse / non-finite-number
   363	    // smuggling through structuredClone's permissive deep-clone semantics.
   364	    if (HI.deepOriginalShapeAudit(g) !== true) {
   365	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   366	    }
   367	    if (g.schemaVersion !== 1) {
   368	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.UNSUPPORTED_FUTURE_SCHEMA] };
   369	    }
   370	    if (!_isNonEmptyString(g.graphId)) {
   371	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   372	    }
   373	    if (g.graphId.length > 64 || !HI.safeRegExpTest(/^graph_[0-9a-f]{16}$/, g.graphId)) {
   374	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   375	    }
   376	    if (!_isPlainObject(g.caseAssociation)) {
   377	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   378	    }
   379	    if (!_isNonEmptyString(g.caseAssociation.caseId) || !_isNonEmptyString(g.caseAssociation.sessionId)) {
   380	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   381	    }
   382	    if (!_isPlainObject(g.sessionAssociation)
   383	        || g.sessionAssociation.sessionId !== g.caseAssociation.sessionId) {
   384	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_SESSION_MISMATCH] };
   385	    }
   386	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.nodes)) {
   387	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   388	    }
   389	    if (g.nodes.length > GRAPH_NODE_INPUT_CAP) {
   390	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.GRAPH_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   391	    }
   392	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.edges)) {
   393	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   394	    }
   395	    if (g.edges.length > GRAPH_EDGE_INPUT_CAP) {
   396	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.GRAPH_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   397	    }
   398	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.correlationGroups)) {
   399	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   400	    }
   401	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.limitations)) {
   402	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   403	    }
   404	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.cannotConclude)) {
   405	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   406	    }
   407	
   408	    // Per-node identity scope + D2 invariant re-validation. Codex D3 R1/R2 closures:
   409	    // the graphId hash is a diagnostic identifier, not an integrity proof; D3 MUST independently
   410	    // re-enforce every D2 invariant on the snapshot it has just cloned. We do NOT trust the
   411	    // hashed projection alone — the projection itself is not the full graph schema.
   412	    var caseId = g.caseAssociation.caseId;
   413	    var sessionId = g.caseAssociation.sessionId;
   414	    var caseLapId = (g.caseAssociation.lapId == null) ? null : g.caseAssociation.lapId;
   415	    var seenNodeIds = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   416	    var nodeIdSet = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   417	    // Build the expected correlation partition from node identities. Two nodes share a
   418	    // correlation group iff they share (caseId|sessionId|lapId|sourceId). This mirrors D2's
   419	    // _correlationGroupKey logic. Codex D3 R2 R2-01 closure: D3 must reconstruct and verify the
   420	    // partition itself, not just validate the listed groups in isolation.
   421	    var expectedGroupBuckets = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();  // key → { memberNodeIds: [...] }
   422	    var nodeCorrelationKey = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();    // nodeId → key
   423	
   424	    for (var i = 0; i < g.nodes.length; i++) {
   425	      var n = g.nodes[i];
   426	      if (!_isPlainObject(n)) {
   427	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   428	      }
   429	      // Codex D3 R4 D3-R4-01 closure: delegate FULL SourceIdentity validation to the D1 contract.
   430	      // SI.validateSourceIdentity enforces: (a) closed key set {caseId, sessionId, lapId, sourceId,
   431	      // sourceVersion, freshness} via Reflect.ownKeys allowlist (rejects extra keys + Symbol keys);
   432	      // (b) caseId/sessionId/sourceId/sourceVersion/freshness mandatory non-empty strings;
   433	      // (c) byte caps (≤512) on ALL string fields including caseId/sessionId; (d) lapId optional but
   434	      // if present must be null or non-empty string + byte cap; (e) freshness must match the
   435	      // contract's ISO 8601 grammar (T separator + Z or ±HH:MM offset). This single delegation
   436	      // replaces several inline checks that were partial.
   437	      var sidCheck = SI.validateSourceIdentity(n.identity);
   438	      if (sidCheck.valid !== true) {
   439	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: (sidCheck && sidCheck.reasonCodes) ? sidCheck.reasonCodes : [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   440	      }
   441	      if (!_isPlainObject(n.identity)) {
   442	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   443	      }
   444	      // Codex D3 R2 R2-02 closure: enforce ID_RE grammar + forbidden pattern + byte cap on
   445	      // every node.nodeId. _isNonEmptyString alone admitted '../bad' style IDs.
   446	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(n.nodeId)) {
   447	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_MISSING_ID] };
   448	      }
   449	      if (HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_FORBIDDEN_RE, n.nodeId) || !HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_RE, n.nodeId)) {
   450	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_ID_FORBIDDEN] };
   451	      }
   452	      if (_byteLength(n.nodeId) > STRING_BYTE_CAP) {
   453	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   454	      }
   455	      // Codex D3 R1 RN-02 closure: D2 invariant — no duplicate nodeId.
   456	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(seenNodeIds, n.nodeId)) {
   457	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_DUPLICATE_ID] };
   458	      }
   459	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(seenNodeIds, n.nodeId, true);
   460	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(nodeIdSet, n.nodeId, true);
   461	
   462	      if (n.identity.caseId !== caseId) {
   463	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_CASE_MISMATCH] };
   464	      }
   465	      if (n.identity.sessionId !== sessionId) {
   466	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_SESSION_MISMATCH] };
   467	      }
   468	      var nodeLapId = (n.identity.lapId == null) ? null : n.identity.lapId;
   469	      if (caseLapId !== null && nodeLapId !== null && caseLapId !== nodeLapId) {
   470	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_LAP_MISMATCH] };
   471	      }
   472	      if (n.identity.sourceId === 'imported_summary' && n.credibility === 'measured') {
   473	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_IMPORTED_SUMMARY_ELEVATION_FORBIDDEN] };
   474	      }
   475	      // Codex D3 R3 R3-04 closure: enforce full SourceIdentity invariants — every identity field
   476	      // that D2 requires non-empty MUST be non-empty + within byte cap. sourceVersion=null /
   477	      // sourceId="" / lapId="" / freshness="" all sneak past the basic scope checks if not
   478	      // explicitly rejected here.
   479	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(n.identity.sourceId)) {
   480	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   481	      }
   482	      if (_byteLength(n.identity.sourceId) > STRING_BYTE_CAP) {
   483	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   484	      }
   485	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(n.identity.sourceVersion)) {
   486	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_VERSION_MISSING] };
   487	      }
   488	      if (_byteLength(n.identity.sourceVersion) > STRING_BYTE_CAP) {
   489	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   490	      }
   491	      if (nodeLapId !== null) {
   492	        if (!_isNonEmptyString(nodeLapId)) {
   493	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   494	        }
   495	        if (_byteLength(nodeLapId) > STRING_BYTE_CAP) {
   496	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   497	        }
   498	      }
   499	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(n.identity.freshness)) {
   500	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   501	      }
   502	      if (_byteLength(n.identity.freshness) > STRING_BYTE_CAP) {
   503	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   504	      }
   505	      var freshMs = _isoToMs(n.identity.freshness);
   506	      if (freshMs === null) {
   507	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   508	      }
   509	      // Codex D3 R2 R2-03 closure: freshness enforcement is now MANDATORY. refNowMs must be
   510	      // available (resolved from opts.clock or opts.referenceNowMs). maxAgeMs has a default
   511	      // (DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS). If no reference time is available we fail-closed before this
   512	      // function is even called — see buildHypothesisSet entry.
   513	      if ((refNowMs - freshMs) > maxAgeMs) {
   514	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   515	      }
   516	      if (freshMs > (refNowMs + (5 * 60 * 1000))) {
   517	        // Reject future-dated freshness (>5min in the future) — clock-skew tolerance only.
   518	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   519	      }
   520	
   521	      // Codex D3 R3 R3-01 closure: collision-free correlation key. Use D2's exact encoding
   522	      // (r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:_correlationGroupCanonical) - HI.stableStringify each
   523	      // component so JSON-string escaping eliminates ambiguity (caseId="a|b" cannot collide
   524	      // with lapId containing "|"). The '|' delimiter sits BETWEEN JSON-quoted strings.
   525	      var corrKey = HI.stableStringify(caseId)
   526	        + '|' + HI.stableStringify(sessionId)
   527	        + '|' + HI.stableStringify(nodeLapId == null ? null : nodeLapId)
   528	        + '|' + HI.stableStringify(n.identity.sourceId);
   529	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(nodeCorrelationKey, n.nodeId, corrKey);
   530	      var bucketDesc = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedGroupBuckets, corrKey);
   531	      if (bucketDesc) {
   532	        _arrPush(bucketDesc.value, n.nodeId);
   533	      } else {
   534	        HI.safeDefineDataProperty(expectedGroupBuckets, corrKey, [n.nodeId]);
   535	      }
   536	    }
   537	
   538	    // Codex D3 R2 R2-01 closure: validate that the graph's correlationGroups EXACTLY match the
   539	    // expected partition derived from node identities. EVERY bucket (singleton or multi-member)
   540	    // MUST appear as exactly one correlationGroup with the same memberNodeIds (sorted) and
   541	    // independenceWeight = exact 1 / count (Object.is equality — Codex D3 R2 R2-02 closure
   542	    // rejects NaN/Infinity). This mirrors D2's behavior: D2 emits one correlation group per
   543	    // distinct (case|session|lap|sourceId) identity tuple.
   544	    var expectedKeys = HI.safeOwnKeys(expectedGroupBuckets) || [];
   545	    var expectedAllKeys = [];
   546	    for (var eki = 0; eki < expectedKeys.length; eki++) {
   547	      var ek = expectedKeys[eki];
   548	      var dEK = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedGroupBuckets, ek);
   549	      if (dEK && _isPlainArray(dEK.value) && dEK.value.length >= 1) {
   550	        _arrPush(expectedAllKeys, ek);
   551	        HI.safeArraySort(dEK.value, _strcmp);
   552	      }
   553	    }
   554	    HI.safeArraySort(expectedAllKeys, _strcmp);
   555	    if (g.correlationGroups.length !== expectedAllKeys.length) {
   556	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   557	    }
   558	    var matchedKey = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   559	    var seenGroupId = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   560	    var globalMemberSeen = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   561	    for (var cgi = 0; cgi < g.correlationGroups.length; cgi++) {
   562	      var cg = g.correlationGroups[cgi];
   563	      if (!_isPlainObject(cg) || !_isPlainArray(cg.memberNodeIds) || cg.memberNodeIds.length === 0) {
   564	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   565	      }
   566	      // Codex D3 R3 R3-02 closure: correlationGroupId must conform to D2's grammar
   567	      // (`corr_<16hex>`) AND must be unique across g.correlationGroups. Two distinct groups
   568	      // sharing the same ID would previously bypass downstream dedup.
   569	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(cg.correlationGroupId)) {
   570	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   571	      }
   572	      if (HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_FORBIDDEN_RE, cg.correlationGroupId)
   573	          || !HI.safeRegExpTest(/^corr_[0-9a-f]{16}$/, cg.correlationGroupId)) {
   574	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_ID_FORBIDDEN] };
   575	      }
   576	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(seenGroupId, cg.correlationGroupId)) {
   577	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   578	      }
   579	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(seenGroupId, cg.correlationGroupId, true);
   580	
   581	      // All member IDs share the same expected correlation key.
   582	      var firstMember = cg.memberNodeIds[0];
   583	      var keyDesc = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeCorrelationKey, firstMember);
   584	      if (!keyDesc) {
   585	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   586	      }
   587	      var keyForThisGroup = keyDesc.value;
   588	      var bucketDescForKey = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedGroupBuckets, keyForThisGroup);
   589	      if (!bucketDescForKey) {
   590	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   591	      }
   592	      var expectedMembers = bucketDescForKey.value;
   593	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(matchedKey, keyForThisGroup)) {
   594	        // The same correlation key appears twice in correlationGroups — invalid.
   595	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   596	      }
   597	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(matchedKey, keyForThisGroup, true);
   598	      if (cg.memberNodeIds.length !== expectedMembers.length) {
   599	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   600	      }
   601	      // Verify members are exactly the expected set (sorted equality).
   602	      var sortedActual = HI.safeArraySlice(cg.memberNodeIds);
   603	      HI.safeArraySort(sortedActual, _strcmp);
   604	      for (var mi = 0; mi < sortedActual.length; mi++) {
   605	        if (sortedActual[mi] !== expectedMembers[mi]) {
   606	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   607	        }
   608	        if (!HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, sortedActual[mi])) {
   609	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   610	        }
   611	        if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(globalMemberSeen, sortedActual[mi])) {
   612	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   613	        }
   614	        HI.safeDefineDataProperty(globalMemberSeen, sortedActual[mi], true);
   615	      }
   616	      // Codex D3 R3 R3-02 closure (cont.): correlationGroupId must equal D2's recompute
   617	      // ('corr_' + _hash64('corr|' + canonical)). Binds the supplied ID to actual cluster
   618	      // contents — caller cannot rename group IDs to forge dedup.
   619	      var expectedGroupId = 'corr_' + _hashFNV64Hex('corr|' + keyForThisGroup);
   620	      if (cg.correlationGroupId !== expectedGroupId) {
   621	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   622	      }
   623	      // Exact Object.is(weight, 1/count) — rejects NaN, Infinity, or any float drift.
   624	      if (!_exactlyEqual(cg.independenceWeight, 1 / cg.memberNodeIds.length)) {
   625	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   626	      }
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    // Codex D3 R5 D3-R5-01 closure: reconstruct the EXACT edge set D2 emits from the
   630	    // sanitized node fields + correlation groups, then require g.edges to match it as a
   631	    // set. D2 emits exactly three edge kinds:
   632	    //   - 'supports'         from each node.supportingEdges entry
   633	    //   - 'contradicts'      from each node.contradictingEdges entry
   634	    //   - 'correlated_with'  from pairs of correlation-group members (sorted lo→hi)
   635	    // The closed enum {supports, contradicts, derived_from, correlated_with, invalidates}
   636	    // describes the universe of LEGAL kinds, but `derived_from` and `invalidates` are
   637	    // RESERVED — no D2 producer emits them. D3 rejects any graph claiming a reserved kind
   638	    // because such a graph cannot have come from D2.
   639	    var EDGE_KIND_EMITTED = ['supports', 'contradicts', 'correlated_with'];
   640	    var expectedEdgeSet = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   641	    var expectedEdgeCount = 0;
   642	    function _expectedEdgeKey(from, to, kind) { return from + '|' + to + '|' + kind; }
   643	    function _addExpectedEdge(from, to, kind) {
   644	      var k = _expectedEdgeKey(from, to, kind);
   645	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedEdgeSet, k)) return;
   646	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(expectedEdgeSet, k, true);
   647	      expectedEdgeCount += 1;
   648	    }
   649	    // supports + contradicts from node declarations
   650	    for (var nei = 0; nei < g.nodes.length; nei++) {
   651	      var ne = g.nodes[nei];
   652	      if (_isPlainArray(ne.supportingEdges)) {
   653	        for (var sj = 0; sj < ne.supportingEdges.length; sj++) {
   654	          var sId = ne.supportingEdges[sj];
   655	          if (!_isNonEmptyString(sId) || !HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, sId)) {
   656	            return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   657	          }
   658	          if (sId === ne.nodeId) {
   659	            return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_SELF_REFERENCE] };
   660	          }
   661	          _addExpectedEdge(ne.nodeId, sId, 'supports');
   662	        }
   663	      }
   664	      if (_isPlainArray(ne.contradictingEdges)) {
   665	        for (var cj = 0; cj < ne.contradictingEdges.length; cj++) {
   666	          var cId = ne.contradictingEdges[cj];
   667	          if (!_isNonEmptyString(cId) || !HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, cId)) {
   668	            return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   669	          }
   670	          if (cId === ne.nodeId) {
   671	            return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_SELF_REFERENCE] };
   672	          }
   673	          _addExpectedEdge(ne.nodeId, cId, 'contradicts');
   674	        }
   675	      }
   676	    }
   677	    // correlated_with from groups with ≥2 members (pairwise lo→hi)
   678	    for (var cgj = 0; cgj < g.correlationGroups.length; cgj++) {
   679	      var cgM = g.correlationGroups[cgj].memberNodeIds;
   680	      if (!_isPlainArray(cgM) || cgM.length < 2) continue;
   681	      var sortedM = HI.safeArraySlice(cgM);
   682	      HI.safeArraySort(sortedM, _strcmp);
   683	      for (var mai = 0; mai < sortedM.length; mai++) {
   684	        for (var mbj = mai + 1; mbj < sortedM.length; mbj++) {
   685	          _addExpectedEdge(sortedM[mai], sortedM[mbj], 'correlated_with');
   686	        }
   687	      }
   688	    }
   689	    // Verify g.edges matches the expected set EXACTLY (no extras, no missing, no reserved kinds).
   690	    var seenActual = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   691	    var seenActualCount = 0;
   692	    for (var ei = 0; ei < g.edges.length; ei++) {
   693	      var e = g.edges[ei];
   694	      if (!_isPlainObject(e) || !_isNonEmptyString(e.from) || !_isNonEmptyString(e.to) || !_isNonEmptyString(e.kind)) {
   695	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   696	      }
   697	      if (HI.safeArrayIndexOf(EDGE_KIND_EMITTED, e.kind) === -1) {
   698	        // Reserved kind (derived_from / invalidates) OR unknown kind — rejected.
   699	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   700	      }
   701	      if (!HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, e.from) || !HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, e.to)) {
   702	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   703	      }
   704	      var eKey = _expectedEdgeKey(e.from, e.to, e.kind);
   705	      if (!HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedEdgeSet, eKey)) {
   706	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   707	      }
   708	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(seenActual, eKey)) {
   709	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   710	      }
   711	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(seenActual, eKey, true);
   712	      seenActualCount += 1;
   713	    }
   714	    if (seenActualCount !== expectedEdgeCount) {
   715	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   716	    }
   717	    // Preserve original endpoint-existence check (defensive):
   718	    for (var lei = 0; lei < g.edges.length; lei++) {
   719	      var le = g.edges[lei];
   720	      if (!HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, le.from) || !HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, le.to)) {
   721	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   722	      }
   723	    }
   724	
   725	    // Recompute graphId — independent authority verification. NOTE: the graphId only hashes
   726	    // (caseAssociation, nodes-projection, edges, correlationGroups). Other fields like
   727	    // graph.cannotConclude / graph.limitations / graph.provenance / graph.createdAt are NOT
   728	    // bound to graphId — Codex D3 R1 RN-01 closure: D3's output must NOT consume those fields
   729	    // from the input graph (they are re-derived from rule firing below).
   730	    var recomputed;
   731	    try { recomputed = _recomputeGraphId(g); }
   732	    catch (e) { return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] }; }
   733	    if (recomputed !== g.graphId) {
   734	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   735	    }
   736	
   737	    // Deep-freeze the cloned snapshot before returning so downstream code receives an
   738	    // immutable view (the clone was mutable until now).
   739	    return { valid: true, graph: HI.deepFreeze(g) };
   740	  }
   741	
   742	  // ---------- Rule registry (closed) ------------------------------------------------------------
   743	  // Each rule deterministically tests against the sanitized graph and may produce zero or one
   744	  // hypothesis. New rules require new module load; runtime registration is NOT supported.
   745	  function _frozenRule(o) { return HI.deepFreeze(o); }
   746	
   747	  var RULE_REGISTRY = HI.deepFreeze([
   748	    _frozenRule({
   749	      ruleId: 'rule_dq_channel_missing',
   750	      ruleVersion: 1,
  1128	        var nn = keptById[s];
  1129	        var canon = _semanticCanonicalString(nn);
  1130	        if (_hasOwn(semSeen, canon)) {
  1131	          _arrPush(rejectedSemanticDuplicates, { fingerprint: _hash64('sem|' + canon), keptNodeId: semSeen[canon], droppedNodeId: nn.nodeId });
  1132	          tally(CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_DUPLICATED_SOURCE_DOUBLECOUNT);
  1133	          rejectedCount++;
  1134	          delete byId[nn.nodeId];
  1135	          continue;
  1136	        }
  1137	        semSeen[canon] = nn.nodeId;
  1138	        _arrPush(keptAfterSemantic, nn);
  1139	      }
  1140	      _arrSort(rejectedSemanticDuplicates, function (a, b) { return _strcmp(a.droppedNodeId, b.droppedNodeId); });
  1141	
  1142	      // Step 8 — orphan edge detection. Any supportingEdges / contradictingEdges target nodeId that
  1143	      // is not in the surviving graph triggers EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN.
  1144	      var orphanDetected = false;
  1145	      var orphanDetail = null;
  1146	      for (var e = 0; e < keptAfterSemantic.length; e++) {
  1147	        var nx = keptAfterSemantic[e];
  1148	        var allEdges = _arrConcat2(nx.supportingEdges, nx.contradictingEdges);
  1149	        for (var ek = 0; ek < allEdges.length; ek++) {
  1150	          var tgt = allEdges[ek];
  1151	          if (!_hasOwn(byId, tgt)) {
  1152	            orphanDetected = true;
  1153	            orphanDetail = 'node ' + nx.nodeId + ' references unknown nodeId ' + tgt;
  1154	            break;
  1155	          }
  1156	        }
  1157	        if (orphanDetected) break;
  1158	      }
  1159	      if (orphanDetected) {
  1160	        return RC.buildBlockedResult([CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN], { detail: orphanDetail });
  1161	      }
  1162	
  1163	      // Step 9 — build edges + causal adjacency (RN-08: proto-null map).
  1164	      var edges = [];
  1165	      var adjacencyCausal = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
  1166	      function addEdge(from, to, kind) {
  1167	        if (_arrIndexOf(EDGE_KIND_ALLOWED, kind) === -1) return false;
  1168	        _arrPush(edges, { from: from, to: to, kind: kind });
  1169	        if (EDGE_KIND_CAUSAL[kind]) {
  1170	          if (!adjacencyCausal[from]) adjacencyCausal[from] = [];
  1171	          _arrPush(adjacencyCausal[from], to);
  1172	        }
  1173	        return true;
  1174	      }
  1175	      for (var f = 0; f < keptAfterSemantic.length; f++) {
  1176	        var nf = keptAfterSemantic[f];
  1177	        for (var sf = 0; sf < nf.supportingEdges.length; sf++) {
  1178	          if (nf.supportingEdges[sf] === nf.nodeId) {
  1179	            return RC.buildBlockedResult([CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_SELF_REFERENCE], { detail: nf.nodeId + ' supportingEdges self-reference' });
  1180	          }
  1181	          addEdge(nf.nodeId, nf.supportingEdges[sf], 'supports');
  1182	        }
  1183	        for (var cf = 0; cf < nf.contradictingEdges.length; cf++) {
  1184	          if (nf.contradictingEdges[cf] === nf.nodeId) {
  1185	            return RC.buildBlockedResult([CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_SELF_REFERENCE], { detail: nf.nodeId + ' contradictingEdges self-reference' });
  1186	          }

   500	  // Manual "last N chars" extraction via HI.safeStringSlice so a hostile String.prototype.slice
   501	  // rebinding cannot tamper with hash digest formatting.
   502	  function _strLastN(s, n) {
   503	    if (typeof s !== 'string') return '';
   504	    var len = s.length;
   505	    var start = len > n ? len - n : 0;
   506	    return HI.safeStringSlice(s, start, len);
   507	  }
   508	  // Direct-loop concat (two arrays only) — bypasses Array.prototype.concat rebinding by going
   509	  // through HI.safeDefineDataProperty writes.
   510	  function _arrConcat2(a, b) {
   511	    var out = [];
   512	    var la; try { la = a.length; } catch (e) { la = 0; }
   513	    var lb; try { lb = b.length; } catch (e) { lb = 0; }
   514	    if (typeof la !== 'number') la = 0;
   515	    if (typeof lb !== 'number') lb = 0;
   516	    la = la | 0; lb = lb | 0;
   517	    var idx = 0;
   518	    for (var i = 0; i < la; i++) { HI.safeDefineDataProperty(out, idx, a[i]); idx++; }
   519	    for (var j = 0; j < lb; j++) { HI.safeDefineDataProperty(out, idx, b[j]); idx++; }
   520	    return out;
   521	  }
   522	  // Direct-loop shift / sortedInsert for Kahn's algorithm queue. Bypasses Array.prototype.shift +
   523	  // Array.prototype.splice rebinding. _arrShift removes and returns first element via
   524	  // HI.safeDefineDataProperty rewrites; _arrSortedInsert inserts a value into a pre-sorted-by-
   525	  // strcmp array at the correct position.
   526	  function _arrShift(arr) {
   527	    var len; try { len = arr.length; } catch (e) { return undefined; }
   528	    if (typeof len !== 'number' || len === 0) return undefined;
   529	    len = len | 0;
   530	    var first = arr[0];
   531	    for (var i = 1; i < len; i++) HI.safeDefineDataProperty(arr, i - 1, arr[i]);
   532	    arr.length = len - 1;
   533	    return first;
   534	  }
   535	  function _arrSortedInsert(arr, value) {
   536	    var lo = 0;
   537	    var hi; try { hi = arr.length; } catch (e) { hi = 0; }
   538	    if (typeof hi !== 'number') hi = 0;
   539	    hi = hi | 0;
   540	    while (lo < hi) { var mid = (lo + hi) >>> 1; if (arr[mid] < value) lo = mid + 1; else hi = mid; }
   541	    // Insert at `lo`: shift later elements right by one, then place value.
   542	    var len; try { len = arr.length; } catch (e) { len = 0; }
   543	    if (typeof len !== 'number') len = 0;
   544	    len = len | 0;
   545	    for (var i = len; i > lo; i--) HI.safeDefineDataProperty(arr, i, arr[i - 1]);
   546	    HI.safeDefineDataProperty(arr, lo, value);
   547	  }

   549	  // Codex D2 Round 6 RN-16 closure: exact per-node byte measurement via captured _JSONStringify +
   550	  // _utf8len. The previous approximation summed _jsonStringWidth across SELECTED fields and
   551	  // missed top-level structural keys ("nodeId":, "category":, etc.), nested object braces, and
   552	  // inter-field commas — combined undercount of ~300+ bytes per node. Codex Round 6 exploit (35
   553	  // x 30 x 225 ASCII chars = ~265 KB actual) bypassed Step 4.5 (~256 KB estimated) and was only
   554	  // caught by post-canonical Step 17.
   555	  //
   556	  // Round 7 fix: build the SAME per-node idPayload shape that Step 16 constructs (mirroring the
   557	  // mapping at the graphId derivation site exactly), run captured _JSONStringify on it, and use
   558	  // _utf8len for UTF-8-byte-accurate width. The result is EXACT for each sanitized node (no
   559	  // approximation). The outer envelope adds a small fixed wrapper constant accounted for at
   560	  // Step 4.5's running-total starting value (WRAPPER_FIXED_BYTES).
   561	  //
   562	  // Sanitized inputs are plain frozen primitives (D1 contract guarantee), so JSON.stringify never
   563	  // encounters accessor descriptors, Proxies, or throwing getters. Captured _JSONStringify means
   564	  // a hostile clock that replaces JSON.stringify after import cannot tamper with this measurement.
   565	  function _perNodeIdPayloadShape(n) {
   566	    return {
   567	      nodeId: n.nodeId,
   568	      category: n.category,
   569	      credibility: n.credibility,
   570	      provenance: n.provenance,

   576	        lapId: n.identity.lapId,

   579	      },

   585	      },
   586	      limitations: n.limitations,
   587	      supportingEdges: n.supportingEdges,
   588	      contradictingEdges: n.contradictingEdges,
   589	    };
   590	  }
   591	  function _estimateNodeUtf8Bytes(node) {

   596	  }

/**
 * tests/r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js — R3.0D D3 · Hypothesis Engine adversarial tests.
 *
 * Sections (per SKYLINE Continuous Resume Directive §7):
 *   A) Functional happy paths — valid graph → hypothesis set; multiple hypotheses; determinism.
 *   B) Structural — output schema, deep-freeze, ID stability, sort order, cap enforcement.
 *   C) Authority — graphId recomputation, deep-frozen requirement, fabricated graph rejection.
 *   D) Scope — wrong case / wrong session, future schema, stale generation token, identity forged.
 *   E) Confidence — duplicate evidence not double-counted, correlation dedup, contradiction
 *      lowers confidence, stale doesn't support, heuristic ≠ measured, low credibility floor.
 *   F) Limitations & cannotConclude — heuristic must declare LIMITATION_HEURISTIC_ONLY; synthetic
 *      must declare LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY; empty rule set produces structured cannotConclude.
 *   G) Validation actions + alternative explanations — fixed enum, structured, linked to hyp.
 *   H) Security — Proxy / getter / accessor / Symbol key / non-enumerable / class instance /
 *      nested class / Array subclass / mutated Array.prototype / cyclic object / prototype
 *      pollution / hostile clock / oversized strings / oversized lists / oversized snapshot.
 *   I) Privacy — output contains no raw samples, no paths, no filenames, no private identifiers,
 *      no stack traces.
 *   J) Mutation coverage — each HI.* wrapper used by D3 has a mutation-protected direct test.
 *   K) HI-only audit — output structure does not depend on caller's ambient prototypes.
 *
 * Style: tests print `<message>: <ok|FAIL>` lines so the per-file manifest scanner parses
 * "N passed, M failed" at the bottom (parseAssertions regex in scripts/run-tests-manifest.js).
 */
'use strict';

var HE = require('../renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js');
var EG = require('../renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js');
var CR = require('../contracts/r3.0d/credibility-contract.js');
var HC = require('../contracts/r3.0d/hypothesis-contract.js');
var RC = require('../contracts/r3.0d/reason-codes.js');
var HI = require('../contracts/r3.0d/hardened-intrinsics.js');

var pass = 0, fail = 0;
function chk(msg, cond, detail) {
  if (cond) { pass += 1; }
  else { fail += 1; console.log('  FAIL ' + msg + (detail !== undefined ? '  ' + JSON.stringify(detail) : '')); }
}

// ---------- Fixtures -------------------------------------------------------------------------
var BASE_CLOCK = function () { return '2026-06-29T01:00:00Z'; };
// Default test opts always supply the clock so D3's mandatory freshness policy has a
// reference time. Tests that need to override should construct their own opts.
function _opts(extra) {
  var o = { clock: BASE_CLOCK };
  if (extra) for (var k in extra) o[k] = extra[k];
  return o;
}
// Wrapper so tests get the freshness reference for free; if a test passes its own opts it
// overrides. Use `_he(input, customOpts)` to invoke the engine via the wrapper.
function _he(input, customOpts) {
  return HE.buildHypothesisSet(input, customOpts === undefined ? _opts() : customOpts);
}

// Captured ambient primitives — used by mutation-test harness so the rebind/restore mechanics
// don't fall victim to the very rebinding they're testing.
var _origObjectDefineProperty = Object.defineProperty;
var _origReflectApply = Reflect.apply;

function _baseIdentity(overrides) {
  var base = {
    caseId: 'case_001',
    sessionId: 'sess_001',
    lapId: null,
    sourceId: 'csv_import_v1',
    sourceVersion: '1.0',
    freshness: '2026-06-29T00:00:00Z',
  };
  if (overrides) {
    for (var k in overrides) base[k] = overrides[k];
  }
  return base;
}
function _baseNode(overrides) {
  var n = {
    schemaVersion: 1,
    nodeId: 'n_default',
    category: 'data_quality',
    identity: _baseIdentity(),
    credibility: 'measured',
    provenance: 'real',
    availability: 'available',
    confidence: { state: 'not_computed' },
    observation: { kind: 'channel_missing', channel: 'brake_pressure', i18nKey: 'r3.0d.obs.cm.bp', params: null },
    limitations: ['LIMITATION_MISSING_CHANNEL'],
    supportingEdges: [],
    contradictingEdges: [],
  };
  if (overrides) for (var k in overrides) n[k] = overrides[k];
  return n;
}
function _buildGraph(nodes, caseAss) {
  var ca = caseAss || { caseId: 'case_001', sessionId: 'sess_001', lapId: null };
  var eg = EG.buildEvidenceGraph({ caseAssociation: ca, rawEvidence: nodes }, { clock: BASE_CLOCK });
  if (!eg.valid) throw new Error('fixture build failed: ' + JSON.stringify(eg).slice(0, 200));
  return eg.graph;
}

  function _incReason(bag, code) {

    var current = (d && typeof d.value === 'number') ? d.value : 0;

Reproduced against `9254c84c0f6bfa0a441b326b6ecac00f63d02c28`.

The six requested hash-valid mutations are rejected: both reserved kinds, missing edge, extra edge, wrong kind, duplicate edge, and self-reference. D3 suite: 152/152 passed. Full `npm test` was blocked by the read-only sandbox’s `mkdtemp` denial, not a test assertion.

Finding D3-R6-01: Edge declaration arrays are not structurally validated
  evidence: [r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:652](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:652) silently skips non-array `supportingEdges`/`contradictingEdges`. A frozen, hash-valid graph with `supportingEdges: "not-an-array"` was accepted with `valid=true`, although D1 requires bounded arrays at [evidence-node-contract.js:227](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/contracts/r3.0d/evidence-node-contract.js:227).
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Require both fields to be plain arrays and mirror D1’s 64-entry/string constraints before reconstruction; add hash-valid malformed-array and over-cap tests.

Finding D3-R6-02: D3 accepts graphs exceeding D2’s authoritative edge cap
  evidence: D3 permits 16,384 edges at [r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:395](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:395), while D2 rejects above 1,024 at [r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1224](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1224). A hash-valid 47-node correlation group containing 1,081 pairwise edges was accepted by D3.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Set D3’s authoritative input edge cap to 1,024 and lock 1,024/1,025 boundary tests.

Finding D3-R6-03: D3 rejects an authentic D2 graph containing duplicate declared edges
  evidence: D2 appends every declaration without deduplication at [r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1177](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1177), while D3 rejects duplicate actual keys at [r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:708](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:708). Reproduction: D2 returned `valid=true` and two identical `n_a→n_b supports` edges; D3 rejected that exact graph with `EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID`.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Make D2 reject or canonicalize duplicate edge declarations, then retain D3 duplicate rejection and add an authentic-producer regression.

Finding D3-R6-04: R6 edge regression tests are not hash-valid or discriminating
  evidence: HRR5 tests replace edges while retaining the original `graphId` at [r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js:1122](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/tests/r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js:1122). They could pass solely through graph-ID mismatch and cover only reserved kinds.
  severity: NIT
  proposed closure: Recompute `graphId` and add permanent tests for missing, extra, wrong-kind, duplicate, self-reference, malformed arrays, and edge-cap boundaries.

Finding D3-R6-05: Governance and source commentary remain stale
  evidence: [D3.json:35](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/governance/r3.0d/checkpoints/D3.json:35) still describes all five edge kinds as accepted; [D3.json:142](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/governance/r3.0d/checkpoints/D3.json:142) still says 148 assertions; [r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:1063](/Users/SKYLINE/Claude/projects/racing-setup-analyzer/renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:1063) incorrectly says there is no per-node freshness check.
  severity: NIT
  proposed closure: Document only the three emitted kinds, update the assertion count, and remove the obsolete freshness variable/comment.

Enumeration and correlation-group order are harmless because comparison is set-based. Singleton groups correctly emit zero edges. The final endpoint loop is redundant, not an independent defense. Whole-graph rejection for self-reference correctly mirrors D2. R5-02 and R5-04 are closed; R5-03’s substantive confidence claims are corrected.

FINAL VERDICT: BLOCK
